**Chronology: Before Singer reaches the cave — date not established**

## HI-001 — The cave before Singer arrives

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-001 -->

The chamber is beneath the Mt. Baker region, inside an ancient sealed cavity near Lincoln Peak.

It is not a throne room, treasure hall, or constructed fortress. It is closer to an enormous natural burrow. The entrance has been buried by geologic change, ice movement, scree, and collapse.

The cavity is extremely cramped for what occupies it. Glaze fills it almost completely in full dragon form, coiled nose to tail with her wings folded flat and doubled back against the stone. The surrounding rock bears old marks from the slow movement of a sleeping creature with building-scale mass.

The surrounding astral environment carries a **Background Count of 4**. It is savage, uneven, and old, pooled most heavily around the buried entrance and strongly associated with Glaze's long presence. It is not an ordinary ward, lodge, or recent magical contamination.

Natural earth and the dense local astral conditions make astral travel through the surrounding stone nearly impossible. Small weaknesses and gaps exist, but the cave is substantially enclosed both physically and astrally.

Outside is high mountain terrain: snow, glaciers, broken ridges, forested distances, and the broader Pacific Northwest landscape.

Glaze is a **Great Western Fire Dragon** and a relic of an age so old that much of her personal history predates reliable surviving records even for the immortal elves and great dragons.

She begins the scene asleep in full dragon form.

She was already drifting toward wakefulness before Singer arrived. The world's mana had risen enough that continued sleep was becoming less attractive. Her initial state is sleepy, curious, patient, somewhat hungry, and bored rather than alarmed or hostile.

Glaze is dual-natured and does not need to open her physical eyes before the astral disturbance becomes meaningful to her.

Her full dragon body is approximately thirty meters long before the tail, with roughly fifty tons of mass. Her hide is obsidian-black with sapphire veining, swept black horns, faceted amber eyes, and a pale blue-white luminosity at the throat. Her scale and mass must be treated as materially real in the environment.

Glaze does not know the modern world. She does not begin with knowledge of current nations, technology, institutions, politics, recent history, or modern culture. Her intelligence and skill allow strong inference, but do not provide facts she has not observed, remembered, inferred, or been taught.

Her preferred communication is telepathy. In full dragon form she should not casually use metahuman spoken languages; her physical vocal apparatus is draconic.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-001 -->

## HI-002 — Singer in the tank

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-002 -->

The tank hums low, fluid cycling nutrients and antiseptic past incisions closed with a precision no human hand could manage.

Singer floats suspended in cloudy green-gold solution, weightless in the particular way only a body with nothing left to prove can be. Tubes thread into port sites at his throat, spine, and both forearms. Their clean, deliberate insertion shows the unhurried precision of people who have done this exact procedure on exactly this body before, and expect to again. His compact, dense frame drifts a few degrees off true vertical, drawn slowly around by the tank's convection current, then drawn back.

His body is a study in surgical perfection. Every incision is tiny, a few centimeters at most. Dozens are scattered with clinical purpose across torso, limbs, skull, and every one of them is already closed, days healed, the nanite instruments having worked with a fineness no scalpel and hand ever could. No ragged lines. No inflammation. Just a faint constellation of hairline seams, silver-pale against his skin, each one so straight and so small it looks almost decorative, like a pattern etched rather than cut. His fine white fur grows in close around them, undisturbed. Nothing here reads as trauma. It reads as design.

His face is slack in a way unconsciousness never quite manages. Not peaceful. Vacant, in the specific manner of a mind that has stepped fully out of the meat and into somewhere else: a sandboxed matrix construct, isolated and inert, where he's running drills against a new deck he hasn't finished learning yet. No stakes here. No signal reaching anything real. Just a man teaching his hands a new kind of fast, over and over, while his body finishes becoming what it's being built into.

Around him the lab keeps its own unbothered rhythm. Each pod stands separate, individually sealed, individually monitored, and the staff assigned to this wing move through it like people clocking a shift rather than witnessing a miracle: a tech checks a readout two pods down and moves on without comment, a healer's murmured chant threads past on the astral and fades, a cart's wheels tick faintly over a floor seam somewhere out of sight. No one lingers at his tank longer than the length of a glance.

On the cart beside the tank: the rucksack, closed, straps cinched tight, sitting untouched exactly where it was set down. Beside it, a folded pair of white boxer briefs waits with the plain, almost domestic patience of an object meant for a body that isn't ready to wear it yet.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-002 -->

## HI-003 — A day passes

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-003 -->

Morning brings a tech in a sealed cleanroom suit, pausing at the tank's base to tap through a diagnostic on a slate before moving on without expression. The fluid recyclers cycle louder for a moment, drawing off the night's filtration load, then settle back to their idle murmur.

Midday brings two staff passing close, talking in low clinical shorthand about a different pod further down the row. A healer drifts through on a slow circuit, her attention brushing briefly across the tank on the astral, registering nothing worth stopping for, moving on. The cart beside him goes undisturbed.

Afternoon brings a partial fluid exchange: a soft pressure shift, a few minutes of faint turbulence as old solution drains and new floods in, warmer, freshly balanced. His body rolls slightly with the current before settling back to center.

Evening brings the shift change, the ambient light dimming a few degrees to mark it. A new tech takes over the wing, does a walking pass of every pod, confirms seals and readouts with a practiced glance, and moves on without incident. The building settles into its night rhythm.

Singer floats through all of it exactly as he began: perfectly still, perfectly healed, perfectly elsewhere.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-003 -->

## HI-004 — The breach

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-004 -->

Three floors above the tank, a diagnostic feed blinks green for the four hundredth time in a row. It is too green and too steady, the kind of clean signal that only exists when someone is feeding it on purpose. Nobody notices. The tech on rounds walks past a monitor showing an empty corridor that hasn't been empty in ninety seconds.

The outer ward doesn't break. It stretches, somewhere along its northeast face, thin enough that starlight would leak through if starlight were what was pressing on it. Something slides through the gap instead, quiet as breath held too long, and the mage stationed in the east wing frowns at nothing, rubs her temple, and goes back to her tea.

A service door that was locked a minute ago simply isn't, and nobody touched the handle to find that out.

Four shapes move into the lower corridor in a formation, not a crowd: close, low, weapons already up, each one clearing an angle the instant before it matters and not a half-second after. Boots that should echo on polished concrete don't. A fifth shape holds the doorway they came through, watching the hall behind them the whole time the other four watch the hall ahead. They don't call out. They don't hesitate at the first junction. They already know which way to turn.

Down in the tank, floating in green-gold silence, Singer's eyes are open.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-004 -->

## HI-005 — Waking

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-005 -->

The tank doesn't open so much as it stops being asked permission. The seal cracks wide under a strength that reads wrong for the compact frame producing it, glass and gasket giving way in a single motion instead of the assisted, gradual cycle the machine was built for.

Fluid sheets off him in a wave that hits the floor before the alarms do. He comes down out of it already moving, tubes tearing free of throat and spine and forearms without a flinch, and for one strange instant his skin catches the light wrong: a fine, scattering glitter of nanites clinging to him like frost, shaken loose in a shimmer as he clears the lip of the tank.

He hits the floor, coughs once, hard, fluid coming up out of lungs that weren't built to hold air a second ago, and he's already reaching. The boxer briefs are on before the cough finishes, one motion, no wasted time, and his hand is closing on the rucksack strap in the same breath.

"MOVE." It cracks out of him at the two nearest technicians, raw-throated. "OUT! NOW! GO."

The door hasn't opened yet. It's going to.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-005 -->

## HI-006 — The fight

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-006 -->

The door goes down and the room stops being a room. It becomes six vectors of intent all converging on one point in space, and Singer is standing in it, rucksack strap locked in one fist.

Two operators peel wide on entry, augmented legs carrying them faster than any unaugmented sprinter could manage, a clean, hard pace that would leave a track record broken behind them, but still a visible notch under the speed Singer's own body is built for, the gap showing in how much ground each stride costs them against how little the same distance would cost him. Short, disciplined bursts, not spray, three rounds, shift, three rounds. The first catches him across the ribs and leaves nothing but bruising under skin that shouldn't hold against ballistics like that. The second goes wide as he shifts weight without fully committing to movement.

A canister skids in from an angle behind him, already venting. It is something engineered, not standard riot-grade, spec'd against exactly his physiology. The first breath of it tightens his throat before augmentation earns its price tag, and what should have staggered an ordinary man drops to a manageable ache in his chest.

Across the room the launcher operator takes the extra half-second a rocket demands, bracing, sighting, committing. When it fires, it eclipses everything else in the room for an instant: overpressure, heat, a concussive wall catching him across the flank and throwing him half a step sideways even through braced footing. It costs him ground.

The mages barely move at all. They don't need to. A short step to clear a sightline, a shift of weight to hold ground, nothing more, casting carried on support that isn't in the room with them, unseen hands somewhere beyond the walls feeding power into every working so the casters themselves don't have to spend past what their own bodies can survive. The first bolt of current reaches for him anyway and Reflecting answers it clean, sending it back down the line to the caster, who goes rigid and drops without a sound. A second casting, a breath later, meets the same fate, with another mage folding to her own power turned back on her. A third attempt gets through partial, current walking up one arm instead of through his whole frame. Reflecting isn't there anymore to argue with the fourth.

There is no opening. The room is too full and the attackers too many, each one covering the gap the instant before it could become one: a wired operator crossing distance in a burst that outpaces anything unaugmented flesh could manage, weapon snapping into line the moment his feet plant, movement precise down to the centimeter but stitched together in discrete, separate beats rather than one continuous line, and slower for it than the man they're converging on. Where Singer moves like water finding the one channel already open, they move like something clocked and metered, with extraordinary speed delivered in clean, individual increments. He reads every one of them a fraction ahead and still has nowhere to spend the advantage. Full defense leaves no beat free to close distance, no beat free to answer.

Monofilament catches the light before it catches him, a fast precise cut opening a line across his forearm he doesn't have a hand free to protect. Gunfire finds him again. Current finds him again.

Five attacks land clean through the exchange: bullet, blade, blast, current, current. Each hits with force that should have ended an ordinary man in the opening seconds and instead leaves him marked, bleeding, breathing hard, spent halfway down before the room is done with him. Two of the mages who started this aren't breathing anymore. Their own power came back at them when it should have passed through Singer.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-006 -->

## HI-007 — Overflow and escape

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-007 -->

A round catches him high on the shoulder and he turns with it instead of against it, letting the impact spend itself sideways instead of through him. His off hand finds the edge of a fallen cart and shoves it half around, not cover, just something between him and the muzzle for the fraction of a second it takes to matter. His knee buckles on landing and he forces it straight again by will alone, weight coming back under him before it fully leaves. Current finds him a heartbeat later and his jaw locks against it, teeth grinding rather than a sound coming out, spine arched half a degree off true and forced back.

Blood runs freely now, from his side, his arm, a line at his scalp. He doesn't look down at any of it. His free hand comes up, fingers curling through a shape worn smooth by repetition across more years than his face admits to, eyes fixed on the air ahead of him where a dark seam has begun tearing open, its edges bleeding light that isn't light, cold that isn't temperature, the raw geometry of somewhere else forcing itself through the walls of somewhere here.

He shouldn't be standing. Pain doesn't reach him the way it should, hasn't in a long time, and that difference is the only thing keeping him upright. His last working stretch of motion carries him one final step forward, rucksack strap still locked white-knuckled in his fist, straight into the tear.

The room's noise cuts off behind him mid-syllable.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-007 -->

## HI-008 — Transit and fall

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-008 -->

Space stops being space somewhere past the tear, folding through boundaries that were never built for crossing, worlds strobing by too fast to hold: a sky the wrong color over something that might have been a forest, a shore where the water moves upward, a court of shapes too graceful to be safe, gone again before the eye can fix on any of it. Astral currents drag him faster than falling, faster than running, faster than any speed that has a name in the world he left.

Ahead, the pull sharpens toward a blot on the astral that shouldn't exist: mana knotted so dense it reads as a wound rather than a place, a shape massive and half-hidden inside its own density, colors that aren't colors bleeding together: a blue like fire, a blue like sapphire, and beneath both a darkness that isn't the absence of light so much as the refusal of it. Reality thins to nothing in front of that shape and then simply isn't there anymore.

It unzips. It always does, at the end.

He lands hard on rock, the impact driving what little air his lungs are still working with clean out of him. He doesn't open his eyes. He can't, not with what's left of him, and so the cave that receives him arrives only in what a body can register with sight gone: cold stone against his back, uneven and sharp in places, biting into skin that's still bleeding, slowly now, augmentation and nanite discipline stretching what should be minutes into something closer to hours. The air is chill enough to raise what's left of his fur, thin and mineral-tasting on his tongue with each shallow breath, carrying the faint mustiness of old stone that's never seen weather. Somewhere close, a sound reaches him. It is not voices or machinery, but something quieter and more patient: water finding its way through rock one drop at a time, echoing just enough to say the space around him is larger than his body currently fills. And warmth, faint and strange, radiating from one direction only, low and constant against his skin, like a coal that never quite goes out.

Blood spreads slow and dark across old rock, finding the same worn channels water has carved over an unknown span of years, pooling where the stone dips instead of soaking in. With it comes a scatter of things that don't belong on a cave floor: a length of surgical tubing, still trailing a thread of clear line, curled where it landed. A torn strip of bandage, unspooling loose across the stone. Fragments of tank glass, catching the amber light in small, scattered points before going dull with settling dust.

The mineral cold of the cave gets crowded out fast. A sharp, electric edge clings to him first, the smell of something that was current a moment ago and hasn't finished forgetting it. Under that, a scorched, metallic weight, the kind that sits in fabric after something violent has passed close enough to singe it, sweetened at the edges by a chemical taint that shouldn't be sweet at all, a taste more than a smell, catching low in the throat. Older notes surface last, the ones that don't announce themselves so much as settle: something faintly medicinal and wrong, clinging thin and slick to skin that spent too long submerged; something sour from a body that gave up more than blood on the way here. None of it has any business in a place this old and this empty. It fills the space anyway, stubborn, refusing to thin.

Somewhere in the black, a drip of water lands, a beat late against its own rhythm, then falls back into pattern. The amber light continues at the same steady output it has held throughout, its glow reaching the same distance across the same stones, a distance that happens, now, to end at torn skin, slowing blood, and shallow, uneven breath. Dust settles where it was disturbed. Air moves through the space in the same slow currents it always has, indifferent to what has just been added to the floor.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-008 -->

## HI-009 — Arrival in the cave

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-009 -->

Singer was badly injured but stabilized rather than actively dying. He had suffered ballistic trauma, a monofilament cut, blast damage, electrical injury, scalp injury, and associated blood loss. His medical augmentations and nanite systems were working aggressively to preserve him.

He was initially unconscious or only intermittently conscious. His visual systems were badly overloaded and required patient, one-system-at-a-time resets before usable sight could return.

Singer did **not** know where he was, how far he had traveled, or that he was no longer in the version of the world he remembered.

His arrival was extraordinary to Glaze because a living being had appeared inside a sealed chamber through a route that should not exist.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-009 -->

## HI-010 — Opening medical and sensory state

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-010 -->

Singer's body was being kept alive by multiple layers of internal support. His Guardian Angel and other medical systems could enter an emergency-aid mode, display diagnostic information on the body, issue instructions through available interfaces, and attempt to recruit outside assistance. His nanite and biological repair systems could stabilize and close damage, but serious destruction to those systems required time to rebuild.

At the beginning of the cave sequence, Singer had severe Physical injury and Deadly Stun. His normal astral presentation was deeply Masked, presenting an intentionally ordinary version of himself rather than revealing his extensive magical, biological, cybernetic, molecular, or form-changing alterations.

Before his vision was restored, the cave was experienced primarily through touch, hearing, scent, taste, internal instrumentation, astral perception when used, and temperature. His smell and taste were unusually capable information channels. Once restored, his natural and augmented vision included hyperspectral perception beyond normal human or elven ranges, including additional orange, yellow, indigo, violet, near-ultraviolet, and near-infrared sensitivity.

The local Background Count severely interfered with magical and related healing attempts.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-010 -->

**Chronology: After Singer’s arrival and before May 23, 2051**

## HI-011 — Physical / Magical State

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-011 -->

Singer had become physically healed from the deadly injuries with which he arrived.

The progression during the session included:
- emergency stabilization by his internal medical systems,
- gradual Stun recovery,
- several uses of the Lightbearer ability Heal Believer,
- a final conventional Force 9 Heal spell after the cave's hostile background was removed.

The final Heal spell restored the remaining Physical damage completely. Singer is physically whole at session close unless the user establishes a new injury.

Singer's medical/nanite systems were heavily damaged earlier and spent many hours rebuilding. They have shown sophisticated autonomous triage behavior, including:
- emergency stabilization,
- recruiting outside aid through displays and canned language output,
- directing wound closure,
- monitoring blood pressure and other vital signs,
- microscopic repair / manufacturing activity.

Singer's vision systems were manually rebuilt/reset one subsystem at a time. His sight is functional again.

The cave's hostile **Background Count began at 4**. Singer repeatedly attempted Cleansing and ultimately pushed the remaining count from 3 to **0** using Sorcery plus Karma Pool.

Singer has only directly learned the first four Lightbearer abilities, despite having Lightbearer Rank 15. Other abilities may be latent and discovered through intuition/use. He has already sensed that additional healing-related Lightbearer functions exist, but he does not automatically know their names, activation procedures, or limits.

Singer's High Pain Tolerance covers 6 boxes for wound-modifier purposes.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-011 -->

## HI-012 — Singer's Manifested Magical Presentation

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-012 -->

Singer demonstrated a physically visible shamanic mask while casting Heal after the Background Count was cleared.

Observed form:
- angel-of-death imagery,
- dark cloak/robes,
- crown/halo of holly and stars or broken glass.

Singer has explained that much of his magic is "practical lies told to reality": concealment, conflict avoidance, misdirection, and sometimes conflict creation.

Singer's normal astral presentation is heavily Masked and deliberately ordinary. His true/unmasked astral presentation had previously shown:
- a large, powerful Nephilim-like form,
- dark robes,
- holly-and-berry halo/crown,
- ghostly lantern and bardic instruments,
- strong self-concept tied to Dark King totem / bard path,
- extensive hidden magical, biological, cybernetic, and form-changing complexity.

Singer later normalized his Masking and explained that he deliberately hides:
- magical power,
- Essence irregularities,
- form-changing / morphic traits,
- cybernetic systems,
- biological/molecular modifications,
- unusual capabilities.

Glaze respects this choice and does not treat penetrating his Masking as permission to pry further.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-012 -->

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-013 -->

Singer has told or demonstrated that:

- avoiding machine tracking,

- creating convincing alternate vocal identities.

## HI-025 — Singer's Phone / Teaching Setup

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-025 -->

Singer left Glaze access to:
- a smartphone / handheld unit,
- linked commlink resources,
- a smart-frame "Call Router" with Pilot 6,
- passive instructional material,
- audio/video/3D lesson playback,
- some Sperethiel-language materials,
- deeper libraries reachable through Singer's linked systems.

Important interface limitation:
- In full dragon form, Glaze cannot vocally speak Sperethiel or English.
- Dragon Tongue is not available to humans/devices and no normal library can translate it.
- Therefore full-dragon Glaze can listen to Sperethiel output but cannot verbally converse with the phone in Sperethiel.
- She can use passive teaching, physical interface methods if feasible, or change to a form with metahuman vocal anatomy.
- Do **not** have devices understand Dragon Tongue unless a specific extraordinary mechanism is established.

Call Router has been used as an adaptive mediator:
- tracks where Glaze fails to understand modern assumptions,
- supplies definitions/context,
- adjusts passive instruction,
- flags older-language misunderstandings,
- logs her interaction for Singer to review later at high speed.

Singer can replay recorded lessons / interaction data mentally at very high subjective speed (he demonstrated roughly 40× review speed).

## HI-026 — Modern Magic: What Glaze Has Been Told

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-026 -->

Singer told Glaze that strong magic returned abruptly in **2011 AD** by the dominant western calendar, around the completion of a Mayan long cycle.

Early changes included:
- elven and dwarven children,
- later adult transformation into orks and trolls,
- Singer and his family becoming elves after extreme magical exposure during the Great Ghost Dance.

Singer was approximately forty when the Awakening occurred.

He described the early decades as chaotic:
- very little trustworthy magical theory,
- huge volumes of pre-Awakening nonsense,
- sporadic useful practices,
- religious/prayer traditions sometimes functioning,
- rapid testing and reconstruction.

Singer believes modern magical scholarship has existed for only about two full generations / roughly 55 years in his remembered world.

He explained that six billion people, rapid communication, global institutions, and intense specialization allowed magical knowledge to be rebuilt quickly.

Singer has professional-level libraries for:
- Sorcery,
- Conjuring,
- Enchanting,
- broadly public magical scholarship.

He estimated the paper-book equivalent of his public magical libraries at roughly **17 cubic meters**.

He does not possess all obscure/private magical knowledge and explicitly distinguishes public scholarship from information held in private-access communities.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-026 -->

- reduced state/corporate presence does not mean nobody watches; local/private eyes matter more;
- a good private base is often a place where watchers **do not care**, not a place nobody sees;
- quiet ownership can mean working utilities, no obvious squatting, deliveries that are not taxed, and neighbors who mind their business;
- shadow economy can function as infrastructure for people excluded from official infrastructure;
- hardship and ruins are consequences, not singular causes;
- a poor neighborhood can still be someone's kingdom;
- doing nothing is also policy because costs accumulate through hospitals, violence, theft, lost labor, children, later policing and further damage.

Singer discussed Tamanous/organlegger economics, Yakuza/Triad movement, Aztechnology's hidden property footprints, low drone coverage, and the social history of Redmond. Tomas remained knowledgeable but bounded to his local patch.

They also passed **Hangbokhan Nongjang** farm operations near the border area. Singer recalled old rumors of Triad/Seoulpa ties; Tomas treated those as old/uncertain rumors rather than fact. Glaze was more interested in people making exhausted urban land productive again.

## HI-099 — Taetzal as state shared-property candidate

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-099 -->

- vehicle/garage access suitable for multiple vehicles;

- most of the building is securely locked and dark;
- a Yakuza-sponsored motorcycle repair business operates on the first floor;
- **New Horizons Development** owns it and is in bankruptcy, actively trying to dispose of it;
- the building's nominal value may be effectively **0¥** because of environmental liability;
- roughly **6,000 m²** of contaminated/toxic water is under the facility/site;
- EPA/environmental enforcement attention is established;
- Singer's sensors detected dangerous industrial-solvent vapor in/near the deeper structure; he suspected TCA/TCE and warned the bike shop to ventilate if they notice the characteristic sweet/ether-like odor;
- records/news also indicated elevated **1,4-dioxane** contamination; exact chemical inventory is not yet fully confirmed in play.

Historical campaign-world chain, GM truth:

- Shigeda-gumi/Yakuza interests suffered badly over the deal and a boss was killed;
- toxic-waste discovery turned the property into a Superfund-scale liability;
- New Horizons had Yakuza sponsorship/ownership in the history of the deal;
- successor gumi interest, pride, debt, or grievance is at that point unknown.

Singer's original 2064-history version diverged sharply: by February 2051 he had already acquired Taetzal, spent roughly a month repeatedly cleaning the contaminated tanks/area with the same general cleaning magic he used for water in Glaze's cave, and developed the site for medical/security business. His version retained computers, stored trucks, used a rooftop helicopter pad, rebuilt upper-floor living space, and even recruited a naga and basilisk formerly used as guards.

Singer and Glaze explicitly discussed whether pursuing Taetzal was merely nostalgia or a rushed decision. Glaze gave a clear **yes** to pursuing it, provided contamination can be controlled, title/liability is manageable, and the structure is sound. Her reasons are present-world utility: privacy, vehicle space, security, and enough room for her to exist in larger forms. She does not want it simply because Singer owned it in another reality.

Singer's opening intended offer is **0.0¥**, on the logic that assuming the environmental liability is itself the consideration. He has told Glaze that even if they later reject it as a home, magical cleanup followed by resale could potentially generate substantial value, but he explicitly values Glaze's time and choice over speculative profit.

May 27 title/property development then adds important constraints:
- Singer had a substantive call with **Anne Mercer** and New Horizons outside property counsel **Martin Keene**;
- New Horizons confirmed nobody realistically expects purchase money for Taetzal; assumption of environmental liability is the economic core, though buyer solvency, transfer costs, title, taxes and indemnification still matter;
- Taetzal is **not** a simple fee transfer: parcel, primary structure and subsurface estate transfer together, but utility easements, tenant access rights and old recorded agreements survive;
- a retained access covenant covers designated below-grade service/storage areas; its beneficiary is a dissolved holding company, and New Horizons could not obtain release; a successor beneficiary could potentially assert those rights;
- Riku's tenancy predates New Horizons ownership; an old lease abstract may share predecessor language with the lower-access covenant;
- Mercer sent the title/covenant package and may later route a creditor representative/secondary stakeholder after Singer provides enough seriousness;
- maintenance confirmed abnormal odor in a first-floor service corridor; New Horizons is documenting it before deciding whether to make an outside environmental response;
- Singer told Mercer his interest is partly technical: he wants the chance to test a new method for desolvating **1,4-dioxane/other pollutants** if a deal advances.

Dillon's parallel Yakuza-history work remains deliberately separated from the property negotiation. His then-existing best lead is **Kobayashi-gumi** because two independent lines place former **Shigeda-kai** people there. One surviving local former Shigeda commercial-property figure also appears against an old holding company resembling a possible covenant beneficiary. **This is lineage, not proof of inherited Taetzal rights.** Singer told Dillon he will ask for the beneficiary information in the morning rather than look too eager.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-099 -->

## HI-100 — Taetzal / New Horizons negotiation

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-100 -->

Singer had a substantive May 27 call as **Doctor James Taylor, CFO — Blue Mountain Holdings** with **Anne Mercer** and outside property counsel **Martin Keene**.

He emphasized that he had not performed a formal inspection and only advised renters to ventilate. Mercer/Keene accepted that as an informal observation rather than free consulting.

Singer offered availability next week and local Seattle meetings if needed. Mercer indicated a creditor representative/secondary stakeholder may eventually want to speak after a nonbinding expression of interest demonstrates seriousness. No EOI has yet been sent in play.

## HI-107 — Singer's time / visibility / family-support conversation

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-107 -->

Singer told Glaze that without her he could easily work **19–20 hours a day** across electronic, physical, social, Matrix and magical tasks, sometimes while lying in bed, because there is never enough time. He said Glaze deserves his time and he would rather give it to her than feed that endless struggle.

Singer also explained that before Glaze he rarely traveled without layered concealment: disguise, magical Mask/Invisible effects, spirit Concealment, computer obscuration, camera destruction or drone hijacking. Recently he has hidden himself far less because, if Glaze is not hidden, he does not want to be hidden either.

Glaze valued the choice but explicitly told him not to confuse standing beside her with owing the world his face. If Singer hides tomorrow because hiding is wise, she will not treat it as stepping away from her. She recognized the deeper meaning as Singer having spent a long time preventing anyone from assembling the whole of him while gradually letting her see the pieces.

Singer clarified that by supporting Glaze he means **like family**: if she does not work, he will; he is not presently ambitious about money for its own sake, but for her sake would "move mountains, even if slowly." Glaze accepted that model. She does not believe either of them must prove they deserve food by earning money, but she does not want Singer to dismiss a desire to work merely because she does not need the income.

Glaze at that point wants something that is **hers** even if she does not need employment for survival. Possibilities she personally mentioned: teaching, magic eventually, languages sooner, enchanting when she understands the market, and consultation. She dislikes the idea of a modern employer automatically owning her mornings.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-107 -->

## HI-108 — Housing / lair model

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-108 -->

Singer taught Glaze modern housing/lifestyle economics: security, entertainment, comfort, space, location, transit, schools and area security ratings all affect cost. He distinguished expensive A/B/AAA areas—where residents partly buy surrounding infrastructure/security—from Z zones, where fortifying one building cannot by itself repair the neighborhood and meaningful improvement requires broad infrastructure investment.

He compared an expensive Queen Anne family home (roughly **3.7 million¥ sale / 37,000¥ monthly wealthy-family lifestyle**) with a shared lower-cost household across the block (roughly **6,500¥ monthly**, one primary renter plus three housemates whose individual lifestyle benefits are lower). Glaze understood shared households as pooling costly infrastructure.

Glaze refined what she wants from a future home: privacy; space; somewhere to keep possessions; work areas for both of them; somewhere guests come because invited; and somewhere that does not become impossible when she wants to be her full dragon self. She does **not** at that point need a literal castle and rejected paying premium school/location costs they do not presently need, though she understood why families would.

Singer noted acquiring a lair/castle may take harder effort; Glaze recognized that her full-dragon requirement is what radically narrows normal housing.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-108 -->

Singer briefly Physical-Masked both of them as lanky brightly dressed ork youths, rode the Metro two stops, fare-hopped while others did the same, and used Cityspeak/metatype mannerisms to demonstrate **being ordinary rather than invisible**. Glaze ate roughly three quarters of the Ethiopian 64 and most/all of the additional meals, finally reaching genuine fullness. She liked several sauces, especially horseradish despite calling it an attack.

Singer was obviously familiar with the restaurant's food, sauces and customs but **nobody recognized him**, consistent with his former habit of visiting under concealment/disguise.

A large troll showed real anti-elf prejudice and told Singer to "frag off, fairy." Racism determination was **Racism (elves) 5**; Singer's CHA 16 offset vs hidden TN 10 scored 0 successes. Singer answered with a wager/arm wrestle. Opposed Strength result: Singer STR 15 vs troll STR 10; Singer 1 success, troll 0. Singer won without theatrics and then let the troll off the promised meal payment. The troll remained prejudiced but gained reluctant personal respect. He is **not a contact**.

Singer and Glaze then joined the Rhino's rough karaoke pit. The room's shoulder-thumps, stolen tokens, spilled beer and light punches were socially ritualized rather than serious fighting. Glaze learned that karaoke works because everyone is allowed to be loud together, not because everyone sings well. Singer and Glaze traded songs until the room filled and they voluntarily gave up seats to newcomers.

Glaze enjoyed the Rhino and said she would do it again.

## HI-112 — VR / Hell Wing 5

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-112 -->

After the meeting Singer had Glaze use the room's conventional VR goggles/gloves and entered the same forest-glade space with her. The gloves provide mild haptic cues and the headset directional audio. Singer stopped Glaze from physically walking into the bed and taught her to move virtually while keeping her meat body still.

In Sperethiel Singer taught her that virtual movement does not need to be scalar: walking is an interface metaphor/crutch, and an icon can simply appear at the next gate. Glaze rapidly began organizing virtual space as information rather than geography and described **architecture as grammar and movement as punctuation**.

Glaze immediately recognized the dragon biomechanics as unrealistic but learned to treat game rules as their own system and enjoyed exploiting impossible flight physics. She was disappointed virtual cattle had no taste through goggles/gloves.

Singer explained trodes as limited five-sense simsense that is dull enough not to injure a normal user, while explicitly stating that he still wants to verify safety for Glaze before she uses them. Trodes/direct neural input would let her think movement rather than use gloves, though ordinary systems remain slower than Singer's high-end neural/cyberware response.

Singer demonstrated that difference by entering a **64-dragon competitive brawl** against top-ranked players. His system overwhelmed the normal response assumptions: motion was fluid and unwasted, attacks missed him by millimeters, and his committed attacks did not miss. The game booted/froze him for an abnormal response profile. Singer posted **"Guess a Fairlight gets you banned"**, deliberately claiming a roughly 2-million-nuyen top-commercial system rather than revealing that his comparable cyberware installation cost about eight times as much. Other players found the hardware explanation easier to accept than cheating.

Glaze understood by then that VR immersion leaves the physical body vulnerable in a way comparable to astral projection. Singer explained practical countermeasures: spirit guard service, condition-based alerts/standing orders, or a tactical computer continuing to watch physical senses. She is interested in trodes eventually **after safety is established**.

## HI-117 — Infinite Warehouse / Tesseract Discussion

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-117 -->

Singer and Glaze discussed their respective forms of impossible storage.

Singer demonstrated his **Infinite Warehouse** ability. He opened a narrow line in space, approximately three feet, placed a pillow through it, retrieved the pillow, and then opened a larger threshold into a gray, featureless “non-space.” He stood partly inside the opening while discussing it.

Singer explained several practical observations about the ability:

- The storage is transient rather than a fixed mundane room.
- He had previously cleared it because carrying stored material seemed to affect or distort his astral form in ways he did not want.
- He believes the destination is metaplanar or otherwise outside ordinary physical space.
- He learned or developed the storage ability after another ability, **Astral Shift**, associated with an old ork swordsman friend who left Singer certain abilities.
- Organic material does not rot while stored. Singer has stored food and eaten it approximately a week later without noticing spoilage or a change in taste.
- Singer has never deliberately placed a living being inside the Warehouse.
- He worries that abandoning someone inside might kill them in a way analogous to some astral or metaplanar hazards, though he does not believe the storage automatically sterilizes living material.

Glaze’s storage was contrasted with Singer’s. Her small copper-scale pouch contains a stable tesseract capable of storing a substantial amount of material. Singer observed that Glaze’s foci remain magically connected and bonded while inside her pouch. By contrast, Singer suspects his Infinite Warehouse may separate a focus from its owner, interfere with the bond, or possibly de-bond it. This remained untested. Glaze was unwilling to risk one of her bonded foci merely to answer the question.

The distinction became important: Glaze’s pouch is an ancient stable magical storage object but is **not** itself automatically categorized as a modern focus, while Singer’s Warehouse is an ability whose metaphysical behavior may be different.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-117 -->

## HI-118 — Astral Shift Discussion

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-118 -->

Singer described his **Astral Shift** ability from personal experience.

He said he can remain in the shifted astral mode for approximately fifteen minutes before pain becomes severe enough to force the issue. He described his practical travel speed as roughly **1,300 km/h**. He can bring other people through with him, but they share the injury risk of the transition.

Singer said ordinary people he has rescued or transported this way have often suffered minor to moderate vascular injury. Singer himself tolerates it much better because of his unusual resilience and body.

Singer speculated that Astral Shift may explain how he escaped death and appeared in Glaze’s sealed mountain lair. He suggested he might have been drawn toward the pooled mana or magical concentration around her location. Glaze did not accept that as established fact. Attraction, resonance, accident, or another unknown mechanism all remained possible explanations.

The discussion also established that ordinary astral travel is much faster than Singer’s stated Astral Shift speed. Glaze did not regard her foci as what makes ordinary astral movement faster; ordinary astral movement depends on the magician’s own magical nature/ability. Singer’s gray threshold and Warehouse do not behave like ordinary astral projection.

Singer said he had spent or sacrificed some of his magical potential on augmentations and had spent more on a precise/fast-motion ability that improved smaller-scale coordination, motion, and multitasking. Singer suggested that without those investments he might have been closer to Glaze’s magical scale if her focus were excluded from the comparison.

Singer also clarified that he can shift other people with him. Glaze’s practical reaction was that such a power makes sense for escaping death or bypassing overwhelming opposition—such as avoiding one hundred guards—not for saving a few minutes of ordinary travel time.

## HI-150 — LICENSING EXPEDITER / BLUE MOUNTAIN HOLDINGS

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-150 -->

Singer and Glaze headed toward the Downtown Library while Singer contacted Bell’s recommended licensing expediter.

The expediter was established as male but **not given a name** during this session.

The expediter explained that he needed a complete filing packet, including:

- Proposed company name.
- Registered-agent information.
- Ownership information.
- Mailing address.
- Signer information.
- Basic statement of purpose/business activity.

He asked what Singer was trying to accomplish.

Singer explained that the company was intended to hold a building with environmental/EPA problems, remediate it, and potentially sell it, use it, or rent units later.

Singer sent the completed application materials and all requested details.

Singer also sent a copy of the **Renraku America Financial Subsidiary recommendation letter** that he had planted earlier through the Finance host.

The expediter advised against putting language like “EPA problems” directly into the corporate-purpose language.

He suggested a broad, normal formulation covering:

- Property acquisition.
- Environmental remediation.
- Rehabilitation.
- Leasing.
- Disposition.

He said the Renraku recommendation was useful because it presented Singer’s proposed entity as having support or confidence from a major local corporate actor.

The expediter quoted approximately **¥2,000** for accelerated handling: clean filing, correct lane, and active follow-up on administrative stalls. If an actual legal objection appeared, he would call rather than pretend he could magically erase it.

Singer did **not** negotiate the price downward.

Singer negotiated for speed and responsiveness instead.

Singer paid the **¥2,000**.

Singer told the expediter to tell him if external pressure or action became necessary; Singer said he had done “pushing” before.

Singer also expressed appreciation for the expediter’s effort and connections and offered dinner after the work was completed, without pressure, as a way to build a professional network.

A Negotiation test was rolled during this interaction using Singer’s Negotiation 9 at TN 2. The roll was narratively successful with a very high success count. One displayed die was incorrectly shown as an unexpanded natural 6, which violated the campaign’s standing “all natural 6s explode” display rule. The narrative result—strong positive reception—remains valid, but the displayed die list should not be preserved as a correct mechanical record.

The expediter responded warmly/professionally:

- He would scrub the documents Monday night.
- He would push them first thing Tuesday morning.
- He would keep his own eyes on the filing.
- Dinner after completion was acceptable.
- Singer should **not** externally push yet; the expediter would specify if and when outside pressure would actually help.

At session end, **Blue Mountain Holdings was not yet declared legally formed**. The filing was in expedited preparation and expected to move Tuesday morning if no substantive objection appeared.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-150 -->

- Surveillance and counterintelligence.

Glaze agreed that she would likely be very good at the role but identified her weakness as **modern context**, especially rapidly changing customs, jargon, references, and jokes.

Mara Velez was accepted as fitting the new rule.

## HI-153 — Singer’s Library Matrix Run — User-Declared Results

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-153 -->

- Unique dragons.
- Dragon images.
- Names.
- Alternate names.
- Related records useful for identification/cross-reference.

Singer then used the **interlibrary system** to access university systems and dig further.

By the time his tally became significant, he had gathered the information he wanted from **six major universities with top-notch dragon research departments**.

The final archive remained approximately **2,300 MP** in total.

The material was not “paydata” in the conventional shadow-market sense. It was valuable as written academic/library information and as a research corpus for later use.

The collected archive should be understood as a broad dragon research library containing public/academic records, images, names, alternate names, sightings, genealogical claims, scholarly arguments, and cross-institutional material, scrubbed of obvious fiction.

The run was user-declared and should not be retroactively re-rolled.

## HI-155 — DINNER WITH MARA VELEZ

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-155 -->

As the library approached closing, Singer let Mara and Glaze continue the tour and conversation without constantly interrupting.

Singer asked whether they should go eat and told Mara that if she knew a good nearby place, Singer and Glaze would pay.

Mara chose a nearby late-serving casual restaurant, described as having noodles, grilled fish, dumplings, and a late-shift clientele. It was not fancy but was good and would stay open long enough for dinner.

Singer followed behind Glaze on the walk, watching for problems.

The streets were active but thinning. No threat at that moment materialized. The route remained lit and reasonably public.

At the restaurant, Mara ordered for the table after Singer gave permission.

The meal included things such as:

- Dumplings.
- Grilled fish.
- A spicy noodle dish.
- Pickled vegetables.
- Broth.
- A fried dish Mara joked was better if they did not ask too many questions first.

The dinner conversation focused on books, libraries, archives, social use of public institutions, and what survives digitization.

Mara explained that libraries are used for much more than books, including:

- Schoolwork.
- Job searches.
- Legal forms.
- Cheap Matrix access.
- Research.
- Shelter from bad weather.
- Meetings.
- Simply having somewhere to exist without being required to buy something.

Glaze observed that this might be more important than the books themselves.

Mara joked that Glaze should not tell the acquisitions department that she agreed.

Singer commented on the **Transformer craze of the 2020s and 2030s**, saying it had flooded the book market with regurgitated material until Browse utilities began filtering much of it out, which contributed to books from that period receiving a bad reputation.

Singer said he had lived at times with dozens of bookshelves and appreciated libraries, adding that nobody could take away his “license to a paper book.”

Mara responded that he therefore understood why librarians still fought for physical shelf space.

Singer largely let Mara and Glaze talk through dinner.

At the end, Singer paid and thanked Mara by name.

Singer said:

> “See you around!”

Mara replied that he probably would, especially if Glaze kept asking good questions, and joked that Singer should bring Glaze back before she decided the library’s cataloging system was a personal insult.

Glaze answered:

> “No promises.”

After Mara left, Glaze said:

> “I like libraries.”

Then:

> “And librarians.”

Singer replied that librarians were a strange breed: often introverts until they discovered they had chosen a job that forced constant interaction with people.

Glaze agreed in substance, observing that people who chose books for solitude then spent all day being asked where everything was, and that competent librarians seemed unable to resist a good question.

## HI-164 — Major session result — covenant resolved today

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-164 -->

The retained below-grade access covenant that could have killed the Taetzel purchase was resolved on May 30 as required.

At 07:38 Anne Mercer, Martin Keene, and Elaine confirmed:
- surviving authority was sufficient for a then-existing beneficiary representative to bind the release;
- the beneficiary wanted one final supervised inspection/removal opportunity through the old below-grade route;
- no continuing access right, payment demand, relationship requirement, or future easement would survive;
- Singer proposed and accepted a hard 72-hour sunset for the single inspection/removal opportunity;
- release papers were to be signed before anyone went underground and held in escrow, with no basement renegotiation.

At 10:30 at Taetzel, Daniel Sato, a Japanese-American representative with authority for the beneficiary at that point, attended with one younger associate. Anne Mercer and Martin Keene attended; Elaine remained remotely available. Sato requested an additional clarification that physical impossibility of access would not extend the 72-hour window. Singer agreed. Sato stated their interest was a secured logistics room below grade and possible predecessor property; he did not claim the dioxane tank, basement ownership, or future access. Singer digitally signed. Sato signed. Keene confirmed execution and the hard 72-hour window.

Singer told Sato he intended to continue Riku's shop tenancy after the present arrangement expired and was happy to have the motorcycle shop as a first-floor tenant. Sato said he would pass that along and stated that his organization had a relationship with the shop but did not hold the lease and was not acting as landlord.

While Singer and Glaze remained upstairs, the covenant-side party completed the inspection, selectively removed what they identified as theirs, left the basement access open, and called Singer at about 11:47. Sato stated there was nothing below that changed the agreement. Their inspection is complete; anything they left is abandoned from the covenant-side perspective. The covenant is extinguished; the 72-hour language remains on paper but the one-time inspection has been completed.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-164 -->

## HI-166 — Taetzel — canonical structural facts added/confirmed this session

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-166 -->

Footprint: approximately **70m x 70m**.

Vertical scale:
- ordinary floors: **5m floor-to-floor**;
- first floor: **9m**, double height;
- 28 stories plus subfloor.

Exterior:
- floors 1–20: structural opaque reinforced shell, **Barrier 32**;
- floors 21–28: continuous mirrored heavy ballistic-glass laminate, **Barrier 8 minimum**, with stronger points possible where specifically established;
- upper glazing runs continuously from 21st through 28th; isolation spacers close the outer lip.

Elevators:
- 2 cargo, 4 personnel, 2 executive elevators;
- all elevator banks reach ground and roof level;
- cargo elevators additionally serve the subfloor.

Grounds:
- southern front doors centered on south face;
- two parking lots east and west of the main entrance, against the perimeter fence;
- overgrown grounds, heavy hedge/fence boundary, gates/bollards/wedge-ramp defenses as in Taetzel reference used at that point;
- north side has the two large roll-up bays and truck/service access.

Interior floors:
- floors 2–20: barren, dusty, open plates, opaque/dark exterior walls, polished acid-etched concrete, dense metal strips patterned through surfaces;
- floors 6, 9, 12, 15, 18 contain massive electrical banks and heavy **600V busses** for machinery;
- floors 21–26: open plates with daylight through mirrored ballistic glazing, high drop ceilings, widely spaced steel-jacketed concrete columns, polished etched concrete and liberal floor power/data boxes;
- 23rd: rare locked 15x15 room with a large ABUS mechanical padlock that has taken a beating; contains a large cache of toilet paper and other paper goods;
- 26th: same open shell, about 12 ft clear below drop ceiling, about 2 ft above it, then approximately 1 ft insulated floor assembly; some panels fallen/stacked; pallets by cargo elevator;
- 27th: similar to 26th, with nicer drop ceiling and more ceiling soundproofing;
- 28th: sealed floor until Singer defeated the maglock at session end; old Dragon Knights living/operational floor per Taetzel reference used at that point.

First floor:
- divided, unlike upper open plates;
- two north roll-up bays;
- south parking/front entrance with Barrier 18 armored door/security trap;
- maintenance, nurse's office, visitor conference room, security entry/main gallery, two receiving rooms, center warehouse/cargo-elevator access, bathrooms, offices/storage;
- personnel elevators face into area behind security;
- ordinary operating contents stripped but maglocks/security hardware remain;
- old servoguns removed, mountings remain.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-166 -->

- Singer started a small vent fan to move stale air toward an exterior vent.

Singer plans to obtain proper protective equipment before remediation. His method: first dilute with additional water, then use a separate segregation/separation tank and repeated magic treatment in manageable quantities. In his prior history it took more than two months; he estimates perhaps one month then. He expects to work until exhausted, sleep, and repeat until complete.

Singer told Glaze that 1,4-dioxane's hydrophilicity makes absorption easy and referenced historically recognized deaths; he joked that it is worse on kidneys than Irish whiskey. Glaze has not yet independently researched the chemical.

## HI-181 — Redmond / E-zone calibration

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-181 -->

Greater Redmond is a densely populated impoverished metropolitan district, not an empty ruin field. calibration used at that point remains approximately **436 km², population 498,000, density ~1,142/km² (~2,960/sq mi), 79% below poverty, per-capita income 6,600¥, corporate affiliation 22%, six hospitals/clinics**. Most of Redmond is **Security E**; Touristville is C; Rat's Nest/Glow City are Z.

For an **E-rated slum**, the institutional position is effectively that many residents are not real people because they have no SINs. Lone Star assigns no routine patrols and makes no serious effort to prevent ordinary crime. Meaningful response generally requires a SIN-bearing victim making a personal complaint, violence large enough to make headlines, or danger threatening to spill into more important areas; when the Star does respond, it may respond at a very high level. Residents therefore rely heavily on local gangs, businesses, neighbors and informal arrangements for practical security.

Do **not** casually invent municipal garbage collection, dependable city services, routine police presence or equivalent normal-city service assumptions in Taetzel's E-zone. Trash removal is private/informal: paid haulers, scavenging, salvage, local trucks, gang/business routes, dumping, reuse and negotiated arrangements. The invented scheduled city trash truck / named "Evergreen Hauling" answer was corrected and is not established canon.

The Barrens should show the futuristic corporate/societal damage of Shadowrun rather than generic noir/grim decay. Redmond began as a dense high-tech suburb/edge city; catastrophe and corporate withdrawal left enormous amounts of built infrastructure to be reused. Expect intact or patched apartment towers, offices converted into housing/workshops/markets, old corporate campuses, warehouses, schools, retail shells, vehicles, vendors, kids, mechanics, deliveries, electronics, cyberware and Matrix-era technology alongside poverty, pollution, weak institutions and selective abandonment. "Squatter" is a socioeconomic/legal condition, not a requirement that every structure be rubble. Ruined/toxic/deserted parcels exist and should stand out rather than define every block.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-181 -->

## HI-184 — Taetzel's neighborhood reputation — do not forget it

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-184 -->

Taetzel is not an anonymous derelict to nearby residents. Six months ago, street people saw a burst of activity: unmarked vans and several dozen workers, followed by squads of toughs brutally driving people away for several surrounding blocks. The building then became the site of a major fight involving **Perianwyr/a dragon and anti-vehicle missile fire**. The roof still carries physical evidence.

Local people do not need the names Kyle Morgan, Dragon Knights or Perianwyr to know the event. The neighborhood-level fact is essentially: **this is the building where people fought a dragon with missile launchers.** It is recent local history, not a faded legend. Different witnesses/gossip may disagree on details, but the event itself is socially salient.

The new occupation is therefore visible as **the dragon-fight building coming back into use**, not simply a landlord reopening a generic office tower. Singer walking casually around it in quasi-military gear, carrying serious equipment, inspecting/repairing the perimeter and treating the place as routine is itself a reputation signal.

The reputation signal at that point is importantly mixed rather than simply menacing:

- the new occupants are extremely capable and comfortable in a dangerous place;
- two separate groups of operators/watchers have been detected/caught in circumstances that could have become deadly, and both groups survived to tell others that the new occupants did not seek an excuse to kill them;
- the Yakuza-sponsored/protected motorcycle shop was **not** thrown out when BMH took ownership;
- locally recognizable gang/business transport can make deliveries without the new owners trying to purge every pre-existing affiliation;
- Singer has explicitly said Taetzel has too many internal problems and too little interest in claiming territory; the contamination/building is the priority;
- local people who asked for work were given paid exterior work rather than being chased away, were warned away from chemical hazards, and were fed;
- Glaze is independently visible as a striking, very charismatic woman with a strong accent who talks to workers, evaluates people, makes decisions, enforces privacy and plainly has authority of her own.

The resulting ordinary local read should trend toward: **dangerous and competent, not predatory; pays what is promised; does not blindly endanger workers; respects existing arrangements that do not intrude on the property; wants privacy; likely knows when people snoop; not claiming the neighborhood.** Curiosity and caution are appropriate. Automatic hostility, protection rackets or territory pressure are not default consequences.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-184 -->

## HI-190 — Contaminated-water infrastructure

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-190 -->

Through the labor screening Glaze identified **Raymond Cole**, a middle-aged human who said he had industrial wastewater-maintenance experience: pumps, settling systems, contaminated-water handling and PPE. Cole later showed Singer concern about the vent exhaust location and a third pipe/containment boundary. Singer told him conventional treatment would be on the order of seventy million nuyen and that Singer intends to use his own methods, but Cole is hired for the conventional infrastructure: pumps, valves, seals, recirculation and maintaining containment.

The first priority is **recirculation of leakage**. The longer-term physical goal is a tank arrangement that can separate/contain concentrated chemical and clean water as remediation progresses. Cole said he can obtain a suit through surplus but will buy new seals. If he cannot source one, Singer said he can order one with roughly five-day delivery.

Cole has somewhere else to sleep and does not need to move into Taetzel; Singer offered use of the old nurse's-office space only if necessary and would prefer not to create unnecessary residential dependency. Cole intends to return **Thursday, June 1** with proper protective equipment and trace the recirculation system against Singer's schematic.

## HI-202 — Singer's high-density tactical remapping

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-202 -->

The walk is not merely sightseeing. Singer is rebuilding a **high-density tactical/orientation map** of the entire immediate area so his systems can offload awareness and detect changes/patterns even when his conscious attention is elsewhere.

He maps fixed and changing features on the same continuum: trash cans/piles, heat sources, potholes, doorways, alleys, fence conditions, parked vehicles, loading points, line-of-sight breaks, vents/exhaust, traffic patterns and occupied/unoccupied cues. Changes from prior passes matter as much as absolute positions.

His systems can correlate patterns that ordinary casual observation may miss: e.g. the same car cycling through the area with plate swaps or color/body-panel changes can still be recognized through persistent dimensions, acoustics, engine/drivetrain/tire/suspension signatures and repeated behavior. Repeated pedestrians, delivery timing, doors opening around particular arrivals and recurring thermal patterns can likewise become automated awareness cues.

Singer explained this to Glaze after joking that he had also been watching her. The important interpersonal/world point is that Singer's stillness or apparent distraction does not mean he has stopped gathering/correlating environmental information.

Singer initially remarked that forty is fairly old for a human by some frames, then clarified his broader numbers: before the Awakening/VITAS era human lifespan had been around seventy globally and about seventy-nine in the United States; VITAS drove averages down severely, while the UCAS is still above roughly sixty-five and climbing back quickly. Singer stated that **he is 95 then**, while elven genetics are much kinder to aging than human genetics, and that orks/trolls have still shorter natural life expectations.

## HI-206 — Durable incident result

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-206 -->

On June 1 an unauthorized Aztechnology military force attacked/attempted to seize control at Taetzel. The deployed force included an **Aguilar attack helicopter, a Lobo APC/LAV, two Kestrel drones**, and associated personnel. Singer and Glaze stood their ground. The assault failed.

Reynaldo Texamachach was killed. Singer identified him and ultimately killed him. The old Perfekto/Mercurial identity history is resolved as background and does not need to be reconstructed here.

Aztechnology survivors were treated rather than simply executed. They were later released from restraints and recovered by the corporation. The Aguilar, Lobo, Kestrels and remaining corporate property were recovered except for dangerous systems Singer deliberately retained because they remained threats at that moment. Those retained systems had become secured in the Taetzel armory.

## HI-207 — Public information / evidence posture

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-207 -->

Aztechnology PR is actively reducing easy attribution and specificity around the event, especially **location linkage to Taetzel/Perfekto and reconstructable timelines**. Public reports may therefore become vague, contradictory, re-edited, de-indexed or detached from the exact site.

Singer's agents preserved originals, edits, removals, reposts, timestamps and gaps as they changed, with Taetzel security feeds available for comparison. Singer/Glaze therefore retain a much clearer evidentiary record than Aztechnology wants the general public to have.

If the settlement is honored, Singer/Glaze have offered not to turn that clarity into a deliberate public evidence dump merely to humiliate the corporation. This is conditional restraint, not loss of the evidence.

Local neighborhood memory is a separate channel. Aztechnology can make the Matrix/public story harder to trace without making nearby residents forget military aircraft, armored vehicles, shooting, recovery crews and the fact that Taetzel remained occupied afterward.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-207 -->

## HI-208 — Settlement — performance awaiting resolution at that point

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-208 -->

Glaze, operating publicly as **Debbie**, led the substantive settlement after Singer recognized that he was too angry to negotiate reasonably. She did not merely repeat Singer's words; she turned a broad idea of local restitution into concrete demands and rejected a vague community-relations fund as inadequate.

The negotiated structure at session close is:

1. **Direct repair/compensation:** Aztechnology repairs damage it caused to Taetzel and the surrounding neighborhood and covers reasonable direct losses/disruption.
2. **Food/homeless shelter:** a real shelter within useful walking distance with actual service capacity, not a one-time publicity distribution.
3. **Water infrastructure:** a potable-water tanker truck, large stainless-steel storage tanks, pumps, filtration and supporting infrastructure sufficient to make them functional.
4. **Medical clinic:** a clinic capable of supporting **eight physicians**, with proper diagnostic/treatment equipment.
5. **Operating support:** the clinic receives **five years** of meaningful operating support so it does not become an empty photographed shell.
6. **PR freedom:** Aztechnology may attach its name, photograph the projects and construct whatever public explanation it prefers. If the agreement is honored, Singer/Glaze will not publicly contradict the story merely to embarrass the corporation.
7. **No Corporate Court escalation:** if Aztechnology performs, Singer/Glaze do not pursue Corporate Court action arising from this incident.
8. **No deliberate evidence dump:** if Aztechnology performs, Singer/Glaze do not deliberately release their security evidence as a public centerpiece.
9. **Noninterference:** no retaliation or manufactured investigations aimed at Taetzel, its owners, tenants, employees, contractors or nearby people; no harassment through subsidiaries; no continuing intelligence action designed to recover what Reynaldo lost or probe the household.
10. **No astral scouting/investigation:** no projecting personnel, spirits, astral reconnaissance, magical "security assessment" used as a synonym for investigation, or astral tracking initiated from material obtained in the incident.
11. **Reciprocal intelligence restraint:** absent another intrusion, Singer/Glaze have no interest in running an intelligence campaign against Aztechnology.
12. **Reynaldo-history restraint:** if Aztechnology observes the settlement/intelligence restrictions, Singer will avoid investigating why Reynaldo was still alive despite the old public history around Mercurial. Singer explicitly does not want an ongoing intelligence war with a AAA corporation.

At session close these are **terms**, not completed infrastructure.

Singer/Glaze have offered substantial silence and restraint **in return for performance**, not unconditional forgiveness. A later violation of the noninterference terms would change the posture and should be handled as a new fact rather than pretending the settlement never existed.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-208 -->

## HI-215 — Stuffer Shack / Chiller Thrillers

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-215 -->

At approximately **03:21**, six armed **Chiller Thrillers** began a robbery at the 24-hour Stuffer Shack at **92nd and Pepper**: **Catcher, SlicerDicer, Static, Zany, Spike, and Wiley**; Wiley was a magician. Singer later tied the gang to **at least eight murders and multiple burglary warrants** in or near Redmond.

The fight lasted **less than three seconds**.

- **Continuity correction:** Singer's first lethal target was **Catcher**, the shotgunner who fired the opening robbery blast; Catcher was immediately incapacitated. Singer then incapacitated **SlicerDicer**. Any earlier narration in which Catcher later fired at Singer is void.
- Singer later incapacitated **Static** and **Wiley** with the slivergun. Wiley's attempted **Confusion 3** against Glaze failed.
- Static fired twice at Singer and missed. Avoiding the second shot cost Singer **5 Combat Pool and 1 Karma Pool**; only the **1 Karma Pool** expenditure persists after the encounter.
- Glaze used **Reflecting** defensively, engaged **Zany** hand-to-hand, and knocked her unconscious.
- Singer finished **Spike** with less-lethal revolver fire, leaving him at Deadly Stun with **10 Physical overflow** before treatment.
- Food Fight conditions repeatedly burst/sloshed merchandise across the scene and temporarily impaired action.

Singer and Glaze immediately shifted to triage. Glaze used **Heal** on Spike, removing all 10 boxes of Physical overflow while leaving him unconscious at Deadly Stun. Singer used **Force 2 Treat** on Catcher, SlicerDicer, Static, and Wiley. **All six Chiller Thrillers were alive when Singer and Glaze left.**

Manager Mr. Nick made the emergency call. The store security system had taken physical damage, and Nick was willing to let the cameras suffer a **"recording mistake,"** but no complete forensic deletion is established. Several civilians left before Lone Star arrived; **Singer and Glaze were not interviewed by Lone Star** before departing.

The gang's running **GMC Bulldog Step-Van** was used to leave the scene. Singer collected the attackers' obvious external weapons and gear into it. **No implanted cyberware was removed.** The Bulldog had become inside Taetzel beside the Westwind with the recovered gear still associated with it; ownership, plates, provenance, evidence status, exact contents, and disposition remain unprocessed.

## HI-222 — Dr. Miriam Okafor / dragon reproductive-genetics project

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-222 -->

The project advanced from preliminary contact to an actual working relationship.

Okafor visited Taetzel on June 3, ate with Singer and Glaze, discussed the research architecture, and left interested in continuing. She knew by then that Debbie is a female Western Great Dragon and that her human/hybrid/full-dragon bodies are transformations of the same individual. She also received substantially more relevant background on Singer: age 95, born human male, later expression into his Night One/SURGE state at that point, extremely deep genetic modification, a history of difficult conception treatment with his late wife, a son born in 2010 who expressed immediately and required surgery to survive, later high-magic expression by both parents, and Singer's status as a fully Awakened magician with shamanic baseline and elven-path practice.

## HI-248 — June 6, 2051 — State Department / BMH writ

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-248 -->

Singer's government-system work caused BMH extraterritorial status to appear as a mutually corroborated fact across several UCAS systems. A State Department analyst noticed that the status was corroborated but that the originating instrument was absent where she expected it.

Singer's systems caught the verification query before it became a broader surprise. The analyst called BMH. Glaze required independently verifiable credentials before discussing restricted material, and those credentials checked out through the government channel.

Singer supplied the substantive position for Glaze to relay:

- BMH was extraterritorial and was maintaining a lawful writ rather than improvising around permitting.
- The sensitive work was a proprietary **Thaumaturgical Byproduct Material Stewardship** spell/process.
- The process was a trade secret.
- Byproduct handling could require permitting because of aggregate magical potential and jurisdictional boundaries.
- BMH also needed to move security personnel/equipment necessary to defend its facilities and sensitive work.
- BMH regarded outside emergency-security providers as a security risk for that purpose.
- Underlying technical information therefore required restricted handling.

The analyst accepted the explanation sufficiently to change the administrative state:

- the technical basis was marked **trade-secret/security-sensitive**;
- the broad interagency verification query was closed rather than widened;
- BMH extraterritorial status remained valid in the systems available to her and was not suspended;
- the missing originating instrument was recorded for restricted administrative reconciliation rather than general-access suspicion;
- writ scope could proceed for **thaumaturgical byproducts**, **controlled magical materials associated with research/remediation**, and **security equipment/personnel required for their protection**;
- **biological material was excluded** unless BMH later amended the scope;
- the writ did not prevent a competent authority with specific cause from demanding category-level verification of a shipment.

The call ended at about **15:43** with no additional action required from Singer at that time.

## HI-263 — SHOEMAKER agreement

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-263 -->

The confidential dragon-interface project was named **SHOEMAKER**. Varga understood the circular Hungarian joke that *Varga* itself means shoemaker.

Singer/BMH and Sound Interface Works established and signed durable business terms:

- SIW would fabricate, fit, warrant, support, and develop the hardware.
- BMH would supply introductions and specialized anatomical/dragon-domain work when actually needed.
- For a direct BMH-referred dragon client, BMH would receive **30% of gross profit** for that client's dragon-interface work for the first three years, then **10%** for another three years.
- For dragon-interface business arriving through the broader dragon referral network rather than a direct BMH introduction, BMH would receive **10% of gross profit for three years** beginning with the first paid dragon-interface sale.
- For these splits, gross profit meant revenue after direct material and direct fabrication labor only; rent, administration, marketing, unrelated failed R&D, and general overhead could not be loaded into the calculation.
- BMH would receive access to the sales/direct-cost records required to verify its share, but not SIW's entire books.
- Developmental non-Western fitting requiring Singer personally would use a **70% BMH / 30% SIW gross-profit split** while Singer was doing the scarce developmental work.
- Once a reliable method existed for an anatomical type, later work would become ordinary SIW work under the normal referral structure.
- Singer's personal fitting work was not the default; measurement protocol, diagnostic kit, and remote data were preferred first.
- If personal developmental fitting were truly necessary, the preference was for the dragon to come to Seattle or arrange transportation/circumstances Singer accepted.

## HI-309 — June 8 daytime — Glaze's external trode and computer instruction

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-309 -->

Her instruction moved from basic machine logic through modern tri-state/analog/LLM concepts, holographic registers, search, computer architecture, programming-language concepts, electronics, Matrix-security structure, and ordinary interface use. Singer supplied a large skill-library curriculum. Glaze chose **Matrix Architecture, Programming Languages, Electronics, Matrix Security**, and then **Cracking** as her study sequence after ordinary operations.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-310 -->
