## HI-314 — June 8 evening — Redmond night market

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-314 -->

Later that evening Singer and Glaze went together to a busy night market northeast of Taetzel. Glaze chose to accompany him after the computer-instruction period and brought her own spending money.

The outing developed into equipment sourcing, local-business networking, architectural-salvage investigation, electronics recruiting, and contact with Awakened people.

Singer wore urban-camouflage military-style clothing with a clearly military assault rifle slung. People in the market generally read him as armed and competent without treating him as actively looking for trouble.

## HI-315 — Restaurant and laundry equipment deal — Luis and Nessa

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-315 -->

Singer met **Luis**, a dwarf restaurant-equipment salvager, and **Nessa**, an ork commercial-appliance seller and repairer.

They established an initial arrangement involving:

- one commercial high-temperature restaurant sanitizer/dishwasher;
- one commercial washer;
- one commercial dryer.

Singer would repair isolated failed parts or assemblies, while Luis and Nessa would reinstall and test them. Singer's repair labor would be credited against the equipment rather than ordinary sticker price, while vendor diagnosis or reassembly errors would remain the vendors' responsibility rather than becoming additional repair work charged to Singer.

Luis and Nessa tagged the first machines for a Cole pickup. Singer also stated that he wanted **three additional matched sets**, for an intended eventual total of four sanitizer/washer/dryer sets. Used, ugly, or repairable equipment was acceptable if it could be made functional.

Luis, Nessa, and nearby vendors learned that badly damaged internals could have trade value to Singer rather than making an entire machine worthless.

Singer openly used **Fix** on worn motors and subsystems and fully restored several pieces in front of witnesses. He explained that magical repair became increasingly difficult as objects became more heavily processed, that microprocessors were especially difficult, and that the effort and recovery cost made mass magical repair uneconomic. He described his intended model as technicians plus ordinary repair and salvage infrastructure rather than Singer personally performing endless magical repair work.

Approximate equipment weights discussed for the first equipment class were:

- old Hobart pass-through sanitizer: about **120 kg**;
- commercial washer: about **130 kg** each;
- commercial dryer: about **95 kg**.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-END HI-315 -->

## HI-316 — Electronics and recruiting discussion

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-316 -->

Singer told vendors that he was building local repair capacity with several electronics shops/workspaces, with planned infrastructure sufficient for roughly **six electronics technicians and two computer workers**.

He described the initial direction as repair and salvage first, with the profit model to be developed from actual operation. He sought both dead-electronics supply and competent hires and explicitly said prospective workers should be warned that the operation expected to identify deadbeats rather than carry them indefinitely.

Vendors told him that dead-electronics supply was easy to source because local shops accumulated equipment that was too expensive to diagnose but too valuable to discard. Nessa, Luis, and nearby vendors were positioned to spread word of the opportunity.

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-318 -->

Singer and Glaze spoke with **Mateo Reyes**, a young elf refrigeration technician who worked mostly mobile from a small rented bay shared with his uncle. His work included commercial refrigeration, freezers, cold rooms, and some building HVAC.

## HI-320 — June 8 evening — neighborhood water feedback

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-320 -->

Singer asked market residents about the Aztechnology-supplied public water tank at Taetzel.

The feedback he received was:

- the water was being used heavily and was considered useful;
- replenishment was keeping up;
- nobody was charging for or controlling access;
- only minor normal queue friction had occurred;
- the main practical problem was hauling water for residents without vehicles, even across only a few blocks.

Singer told locals that the water existed while contamination from the building was being cleaned and that local residents otherwise would not receive the EPA-style notices surrounding the property.

The practical local judgment conveyed to him was that people wanted the building cleaned up and the water kept flowing, and that both would be remembered.

Singer discussed the possibility of converting or diverting Taetzel's treatment-system tanker into a longer-term neighborhood water-supply vehicle after cleanup.

Singer also instructed that settlement-water demands be limited to water for the two relevant locations once per day, with the external public tank receiving priority and the arrangement abandoned if problems arose.

## HI-322 — Celia

Singer and Glaze approached the fourth Awakened person Glaze had noticed at the Redmond night market, **Celia**, an independent magical environmental consultant. They introduced themselves as **James** and **Debbie**.

## HI-323 — Ramos Architectural Salvage

<!-- POTTERY-RAG-BEGIN HI-323 -->

Singer negotiated with Ramos Architectural Salvage and paid **3,800¥** for all material Ramos could positively identify as having come from Taetzel. The purchase covered remaining doors, frames, office glass, ceiling grid, cable/conduit raceway, fixtures, built-ins, and unusual custom trim subject to positive identification.

Ramos agreed to inventory and tag the purchased material and provide reasonable temporary yard storage. If another buyer sought a purchased piece before Singer removed it, Ramos would call first and any resale or split would require Singer's agreement. Local delivery was quoted at **150¥ for the first flatbed load and 100¥ for each additional load**, with difficult glass or lifting to be discussed separately.

Ramos's nephew remembered Taetzel's compact autonomous pallet movers, designed to carry roughly one- to two-tonne pallets through the freight-elevator routes. He believed they had been sold as a batch early in liquidation, possibly to **Cascade Automated Handling** in Kent or Tukwila, but his company-name memory and the machines' later disposition were uncertain.

By approximately **22:23**, Singer and Glaze had finished the Ramos transaction and the Celia conversation and were leaving the construction-salvage rows together.
